{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /logs/agent/sessions/skills/discover-important-function\n\n# Fuzz Target Localizer\n\n## Purpose\n\nThis skill helps an agent quickly narrow a Python repository down to a small set of high-value fuzz targets. It produces:\n\n- A ranked list of important files\n- A ranked list of important functions and methods\n- A summary of existing unit tests and inferred oracles\n- A final shortlist of functions-under-test (FUTs) for fuzzing, each with a structured \"note to self\" for follow-up actions\n\n## When to use\n\nUse this skill when the user asks to:\n\n- Find the best functions to fuzz in a Python package/library\n- Identify parsers, decoders, validators, or boundary code that is fuzz-worthy\n- Decide what to fuzz based on existing test coverage and API surface\n- Produce a structured shortlist of fuzz targets with harness guidance\n- Automating testing pipeline setup for a new or existing Python project.\n\nDo not use this skill when the user primarily wants to fix a specific bug,\nrefactor code, or implement a harness immediately\n(unless they explicitly ask for target selection first).\n\n## Inputs expected from the environment\n\nThe agent should assume access to:\n\n- Repository filesystem\n- Ability to read files\n- Ability to run local commands (optional but recommended)\n\nPreferred repository listing command:\n\n- Use `tree` to get a fast, high-signal view of repository structure (limit depth if needed).\n\nIf `tree` is not available, fall back to a recursive listing via other standard shell tooling.\n\n## Outputs\n\nProduce result:\n\nA report in any format with the following information: - Localize important files - Localize important functions - Summarize existing unit tests - Decide function under test for fuzzing\nThis file should be placed in the root of the repository as `APIs.txt`,\nwhich servers as the guidelines for future fuzzing harness implementation.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Prefer analysis and reporting over code changes.\n- Do not modify source code unless the user explicitly requests changes.\n- If running commands could be disruptive, default to read-only analysis.\n- Avoid assumptions about runtime behavior; base conclusions on code and tests.\n- Explicitly consider existing tests in the repo when selecting targets and deciding harness shape.\n- Keep the final FUT shortlist small (typically 1–5).\n\n---\n\n# Localize important files\n\n## Goal\n\nProduce a ranked list of files that are most likely to contain fuzz-worthy logic: parsing, decoding, deserialization, validation, protocol handling, file/network boundaries, or native bindings.\n\n## Procedure\n\n1. Build a repository map\n    - Start with a repository overview using `tree` to identify:\n        - Root packages and layouts (src/ layout vs flat layout)\n        - Test directories and configs\n        - Bindings/native directories\n        - Examples, docs, and tooling directories\n    - Identify packaging and metadata files such as:\n        - pyproject.toml\n        - setup.cfg\n        - setup.py\n    - Identify test configuration and entry points:\n        - tests/\n        - conftest.py\n        - pytest.ini\n        - tox.ini\n        - noxfile.py\n\n2. Exclude low-value areas\n    - Skip: virtual environments, build outputs, vendored code, documentation-only directories, examples-only directories, generated files.\n\n3. Score files using explainable heuristics\n   Assign a file a higher score when it matches more of these indicators:\n    - Public API exposure: **init**.py re-exports, **all**, api modules\n    - Input boundary keywords in file path or symbols: parse, load, dump, decode, encode, deserialize, serialize, validate, normalize, schema, protocol, message\n    - Format handlers: json, yaml, xml, csv, toml, protobuf, msgpack, pickle\n    - Regex-heavy or templating-heavy code\n    - Native boundaries: ctypes, cffi, cython, extension modules, bindings\n    - Central modules: high import fan-in across the package\n    - Test adjacency: directly imported or heavily referenced by tests\n\n4. Rank and select\n    - Produce a Top-N list (default 10–30) with a short rationale per file.\n\n## Output format\n\nFor each file:\n\n- path\n- score (relative, not necessarily normalized)\n- rationale (2–5 bullets)\n- indicators_hit (list)\n\n---\n\n# Localize important functions\n\n## Goal\n\nFrom the important files, identify and rank functions or methods that are strong fuzz targets while minimizing full-body reading until needed.\n\n## Approach 1: AST-based header and docstring scan\n\nFor each localized Python file, parse it using Python’s `ast` module and extract only:\n\n- Module docstring\n- Function and async function headers (name, args, defaults, annotations, decorators)\n- Class headers and method headers\n- Docstrings for modules, classes, and functions/methods\n\nDo not read full function bodies during the initial pass unless needed for disambiguation or final selection.\n\n### Procedure\n\n1. Build per-file declarations via AST\n    - Parse the file with `ast`\n    - Enumerate:\n        - Top-level functions\n        - Classes and their methods\n        - Nested functions only if they are likely to be directly fuzzable via an exposed wrapper\n    - For each symbol, collect:\n        - Fully-qualified name\n        - Signature details (as available from AST)\n        - Decorators\n        - Docstring (if present)\n        - Location information (file, line range if available)\n\n2. Generate an initial candidate set using headers and docstrings\n   Prioritize functions/methods that:\n    - Accept bytes, str, file-like objects, dicts, or user-controlled payloads\n    - Convert between representations (raw ↔ structured)\n    - Perform validation, normalization, parsing, decoding, deserialization\n    - Touch filesystem/network/protocol boundaries\n    - Call into native extensions or bindings\n    - Clearly document strictness, schemas, formats, or error conditions\n\n3. Use tests to refine candidate selection early\n    - Before reading full bodies, check if tests reference these functions/modules:\n        - Direct imports in tests\n        - Fixtures that exercise particular entry points\n        - Parameterizations over formats and inputs\n    - Down-rank candidates that are already well-covered unless they are high-risk boundaries (parsers/native bindings).\n\n4. Confirm with targeted reading only for top candidates\n   For the top candidates (typically 10–20), read the full function bodies and capture:\n    - Preconditions and assumptions\n    - Internal helpers called\n    - Error handling style and exception types\n    - Any obvious invariants and postconditions\n    - Statefulness and global dependencies\n\n5. Rank and shortlist\n   Rank candidates using an explainable rubric:\n    - Input surface and reachability\n    - Boundary risk (parsing/decoding/native)\n    - Structural complexity (from targeted reading only)\n    - Existing test coverage strength and breadth\n    - Ease of harnessing\n\n### Output format\n\nFor each function/method:\n\n- qualname\n- file\n- line_range (if available)\n- score (relative)\n- rationale (2–6 bullets)\n- dependencies (key helpers, modules, external state)\n- harnessability (low/medium/high)\n\n## Approach 2: Scanning all important files yourself\n\nFor each localized Python file, read the file contents directly to extract:\n\n- Module docstring and overall structure\n- Function and async function definitions (name, args, defaults, annotations, decorators)\n- Class definitions and their methods\n- Full function bodies and implementation details\n- Docstrings for modules, classes, and functions/methods\n\nThis approach reads complete file contents, allowing for deeper analysis at the cost of higher token usage.\n\n### Procedure\n\n1. Read important files sequentially\n    - For each file from the important files list, read the full contents.\n    - Extract by direct inspection:\n        - Top-level functions and their complete implementations\n        - Classes and their methods with full bodies\n        - Nested functions if they are exposed or called by public APIs\n    - For each symbol, collect:\n        - Fully-qualified name\n        - Complete signature (from source text)\n        - Decorators\n        - Docstring (if present)\n        - Full function body\n        - Location information (file, approximate line range)\n\n2. Generate an initial candidate set using full source analysis\n   Prioritize functions/methods that:\n    - Accept bytes, str, file-like objects, dicts, or user-controlled payloads\n    - Convert between representations (raw ↔ structured)\n    - Perform validation, normalization, parsing, decoding, deserialization\n    - Touch filesystem/network/protocol boundaries\n    - Call into native extensions or bindings\n    - Contain complex control flow, loops, or recursive calls\n    - Handle exceptions or edge cases\n    - Clearly document strictness, schemas, formats, or error conditions\n\n3. Analyze implementation details from full bodies\n   For each candidate function, inspect the body for:\n    - Preconditions and assumptions (explicit checks, assertions, early returns)\n    - Internal helpers called and their purposes\n    - Error handling style and exception types raised\n    - Invariants and postconditions (explicit or implicit)\n    - Statefulness and global dependencies\n    - Input transformations and data flow\n    - Native calls or external process invocations\n    - Resource allocation and cleanup patterns\n\n4. Use tests to refine candidate selection\n    - Check if tests reference these functions/modules:\n        - Direct imports in tests\n        - Fixtures that exercise particular entry points\n        - Parameterizations over formats and inputs\n    - Down-rank candidates that are already well-covered unless they are high-risk boundaries (parsers/native bindings).\n    - Note which aspects of each function are tested vs untested.\n\n5. Rank and shortlist\n   Rank candidates using an explainable rubric:\n    - Input surface and reachability\n    - Boundary risk (parsing/decoding/native)\n    - Structural complexity (from full body analysis)\n    - Existing test coverage strength and breadth\n    - Ease of harnessing\n    - Observable implementation risks (unsafe operations, unchecked inputs, complex state)\n\n### Output format\n\nFor each function/method:\n\n- qualname\n- file\n- line_range (if available)\n- score (relative)\n- rationale (2–6 bullets)\n- dependencies (key helpers, modules, external state)\n- harnessability (low/medium/high)\n- implementation_notes (key observations from body analysis)\n\n---\n\n# Summarize existing unit tests\n\n## Goal\n\nSummarize what is already tested, infer test oracles, and identify gaps that fuzzing can complement.\n\n## Hard requirement\n\nAlways inspect and incorporate existing tests in the repository when:\n\n- Ranking functions\n- Selecting FUTs\n- Designing input models and oracles\n- Proposing seed corpus sources\n\n## Procedure\n\n1. Inventory tests\n    - Locate tests and their discovery configuration (pytest.ini, pyproject.toml, tox.ini, noxfile.py).\n    - Enumerate test modules and map them to source modules via imports.\n    - Identify shared fixtures and data factories (conftest.py, fixture files, test utilities).\n\n2. Summarize test intent\n   For each test module:\n    - What behaviors are asserted\n    - What inputs are used\n    - What exceptions are expected\n    - What invariants are implied\n\n3. Infer oracles and properties\n   Common fuzz-friendly oracles include:\n    - Round-trip properties\n    - Idempotence of normalization\n    - Parser consistency across equivalent inputs\n    - Deterministic output given deterministic input\n    - No-crash and no-hang for malformed inputs\n\n4. Identify coverage gaps\n    - FUT candidates with no direct tests\n    - Input classes not covered by tests (size extremes, malformed encodings, deep nesting, edge unicode, invalid schemas)\n    - Code paths guarded by complex conditionals or exception handlers with no tests\n\n## Output format\n\n- test_map: module_under_test → tests → asserted behaviors\n- inferred_oracles: list of reusable invariants with the functions they apply to\n- gaps: ranked list of untested or weakly-tested candidates\n\n---\n\n# Decide function under test for fuzzing\n\n## Goal\n\nSelect a final set of FUTs (typically 1–5) and produce a structured \"note to self\" for each FUT so the agent can proceed to harness implementation, corpus seeding, and fuzz execution.\n\n## Selection criteria\n\nPrefer FUTs that maximize:\n\n- Security/robustness payoff (parsing, decoding, validation, native boundary)\n- Reachability with minimal setup\n- Low existing test assurance or narrow test input coverage\n- High fuzzability (simple input channel, clear oracle or crash-only target)\n\nExplicitly weigh:\n\n- What tests already cover (and what they do not)\n- What seeds can be extracted from tests, fixtures, and sample data\n\n## Required \"note to self\" template\n\nFor each selected FUT, produce exactly this structure:\n\nFuzzing Target Note\n\n- Target:\n- File / location:\n- Why this target:\n    - Input surface:\n    - Boundary or native considerations:\n    - Complexity or path depth:\n    - Current test gaps:\n- Callable contract:\n    - Required imports or initialization:\n    - Preconditions:\n    - Determinism concerns:\n    - External dependencies:\n- Input model:\n    - Primary payload type:\n    - Decoding or parsing steps:\n    - Constraints to respect:\n    - Edge classes to emphasize:\n- Oracles:\n    - Must-hold properties:\n    - Acceptable exceptions:\n    - Suspicious exceptions:\n    - Crash-only vs correctness-checking:\n- Harness plan:\n    - Recommended approach:\n    - Minimal harness signature:\n    - Seed corpus ideas:\n    - Timeouts and resource limits:\n- Risk flags:\n    - Native extension involved:\n    - Potential DoS paths:\n    - External I/O:\n- Next actions:\n    1.\n    2.\n    3.\n    4.\n\n## Output format\n\n- selected_futs: list of chosen FUTs with brief justification\n- notes_to_self: one \"Fuzzing Target Note\" per FUT\n\n---\n\n# Final JSON block\n\nAt the end of the report, include a JSON object with:\n\n- important_files: [{path, score, rationale, indicators_hit}]\n- important_functions: [{qualname, file, line_range, score, rationale, dependencies, harnessability}]\n- test_summary: {test_map, inferred_oracles, gaps}\n- selected_futs: [{qualname, file, line_range, justification}]\n- notes_to_self: [{target_qualname, note}]\n\n\nARGUMENTS: /app/arrow"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8fb0eb86-8b0c-4a5e-8b9f-c7b21b1f24a0","uuid":"cddfd51a-dea5-4ae3-973a-9c7e199ca157","isSynthetic":true}

Pattern[str]]:\n   442→        \"\"\"\n   443→        Generates a regular expression pattern from a format string.\n   444→\n   445→        :param fmt: The format string to convert into a regular expression pattern.\n   446→        :type fmt: str\n   447→        :returns: A tuple containing a list of format tokens and the corresponding regular expression pattern.\n   448→        :rtype: Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]\n   449→        :raises ParserError: If an unrecognized token is encountered in the format string.\n   450→        \"\"\"\n   451→        # fmt is a string of '(?P<YYYY>\\d{4})-(?P<MM>\\d{2})-(?P<DD>\\d{2})'\n   455→        tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE] = []\n   456→        offset = 0\n   457→\n   458→        # Escape all special RegEx chars\n   459→ 464→        # Any number of S is the same as one.\n   465→        # TODO: allow users to specify the number of digits to parse\n   466→        escaped_fmt = re.sub(r\"S+\", \"S\", escaped_fmt)\n   467→\n   468→        escaped_data = re.findall(self._ESCAPE_RE, fmt)\n   469→\n   470→        fmt_pattern = escaped_fmt\n   471→\n   472→        for m in self._FORMAT_RE.finditer(escaped_fmt):\n   473→            token: _FORMAT_TYPE = cast(_FORMAT_TYPE, m.group(0))\n   474→            try:\n   475→                input_re = self._input_re_map[token]\n   476→            except KeyError:\n   477→                raise ParserError(f\"Unrecognized token {token!r}.\")\n   478→            input_pattern = f\"(?P<{token}>{input_re.pattern})\"\n   479→            tokens.append(token)\n   480→ We keep the difference in the offset variable.\n   482→            # This works because the string is scanned left-to-right and matches\n   483→            # are returned in the order found by finditer.\n   484→            fmt_pattern = (\n   485→                fmt_pattern[: m.start() + offset]\n   486→                + input_pattern\n   487→                + fmt_pattern[m.end() + offset :]\n   488→            )\n   489→            offset += len(input_pattern) - (m.end() - m.start())\n   490→\n   491→        final_fmt_pattern = \"\"\n   492→        split_fmt = fmt_pattern.split(r\"\\#\")\n   493→\n   494→        # Due to the way Python splits, 'split_fmt' will always be longer\n   495→        for i in range(len(split_fmt)):\n   496→            final_fmt_pattern += split_fmt[i]\n   497→            if i < len(escaped_data):\n   498→                final_fmt_pattern += escaped_data[i][1:-1]\n   499→\n   500→        # Wrap final_fmt_pattern in a custom word boundary to strictly\n   501→        # match the formatting pattern and filter out date and time formats\n   502→        # that include junk such as: blah1998-09-12 blah, blah 1998-09-12blah,\n   503→        # blah1998-09-12blah. The custom word boundary matches every character\n   504→        # that is not a whitespace character to allow for searching for a date\n   505→        # and time string in a natural language sentence. Therefore, searching\n   506→        # for a string of the form YYYY-MM-DD in \"blah 1998-09-12 blah\" will\n   507→        # work properly.\n   508→        # Certain punctuation before or after the target pattern such as\n   509→        # \"1998-09-12,\" is permitted. For the full list of valid punctuation,\n   510→        # see the documentation.\n   511→\n   512→        starting_word_boundary = (\n   513→            r\"(?<!\\S\\S)\"  # Don't have two consecutive non-whitespace characters. datetime string (default is False).\n        :type datetime_string: str\n        :type normalize_whitespace: bool\n        :returns: The parsed datetime object.\n        :rtype: datetime\n        :raises ParserError: If the datetime string is not in a valid ISO 8601-like format.\n\n        Usage::\n        >>> import arrow.parser\n        >>> re.sub(r\"\\s+\", \" \", datetime_string.strip())\n\n        has_space_divider = \" \" in datetime_string\n        has_t_divider = \"T\" in datetime_string\n\n        num_spaces = datetime_string.count(\" \")\n        if has_space_divider and num_spaces != 1 or has_t_divider and num_spaces > 0:\n            raise ParserError(\n                f\"Expected an ISO 8601-like string, but was given {datetime_string!r}. \"\n                \"Try passing in a format string to resolve this.\"\n            )\n\n        has_time = has_space_divider or has_t_divider\n        has_tz = False\n\n        # date formats (ISO 8601 and others) to test against\n        # NOTE: YYYYMM is omitted to avoid confusion with YYMMDD (no longer part of ISO 8601, but is still often used)\n        formats = [\n            \"YYYY-MM-DD\",\n            \"YYYY-M-DD\",\n            \"YYYY-M-D\",\n            \"YYYY/MM/DD\",\n            \"YYYY/M/DD\",\n            \"YYYY/M/D\",\n            \"YYYY.MM.DD\",\n            \"YYYY.M.DD\",\n            \"YYYY.M.D\",\n            \"YYYYMMDD\",\n            \"YYYY-DDDD\",\n            \"YYYYDDDD\",\n            \"YYYY-MM\",\n            \"YYYY/MM\",\n            \"YYYY.MM\",\n            \"YYYY\",\n            \"W\",\n        ]\n\n        if has_time:\n            if has_space_divider:\n                date_string, time_string = datetime_string.split(\" \", 1)\n            else:\n                date_string, time_string = datetime_string.split(\"T\", 1)\n\n            time_parts = re.split(\n                r\"[\\+\\-Z]\", time_string, maxsplit=1, flags=re.IGNORECASE\n            )\n\n            time_components: Optional[Match[str]] = self._TIME_RE.match(time_parts[0])\n\n            if time_components is None:\n                raise ParserError(\n                    \"Invalid time component provided. \"\n                    \"Please specify a format or provide a valid time component in the basic or extended ISO 8601 time format.\"\n                )\n\n            (\n                hours,\n                minutes,\n                seconds,\n                subseconds_sep,\n                subseconds,\n            ) = time_components.groups()\n\n            has_tz = len(time_parts) == 2\n            has_minutes = minutes is not None\n            has_seconds = seconds is not None\n            has_subseconds = subseconds is not None\n\n            is_basic_time_format = \":\" not in time_parts[0]\n            tz_format = \"Z\"\n\n            # use 'ZZ' token instead since tz offset is present in non-basic format\n            if has_tz and \":\" in time_parts[1]:\n                tz_format = \"ZZ\"\n\n            time_sep = \"\" if is_basic_time_format else \":\"\n\n            if has_subseconds:\n                time_string = \"HH{time_sep}mm{time_sep}ss{subseconds_sep}S\".format(\n                    time_sep=time_sep, subseconds_sep=subseconds_sep\n                )\n            elif has_seconds:\n                time_string = \"HH{time_sep}mm{time_sep}ss\".format(time_sep=time_sep)\n            elif has_minutes:\n                time_string = f\"HH{time_sep}mm\"\n            else:\n                time_string = \"HH\"\n\n            if has_space_divider:\n                formats = [f\"{f} {time_string}\" for f in formats]\n            else:\n                formats = to be parsed\n            formats = [f\"{f}{tz_format}\" for f in formats]\n\n        return self._parse_multiformat(datetime_string, formats)\n\n    def parse(\n        self,\n        datetime_string: str,\n        fmt: Union[List[str], str],\n        normalize_whitespace: bool = False,\n    ) -> datetime:\n        \"\"\"\n        Parses a datetime string using a specified format.\n\n        :param datetime_string: The datetime string to parse.\n        :param fmt: The format string or list of format strings to use for parsing.\n        :param normalize_whitespace: Whether to normalize whitespace in the datetime string (default is False).\n        :type datetime_string: str\n        :type fmt: Union[List[str], str]\n        :type normalize_whitespace: bool\n        :returns: The parsed datetime object.\n        :rtype: datetime\n        :raises ParserMatchError: If the datetime string does not match the specified format.\n\n        Usage::\n\n        >>> import arrow.parser\n        >>> arrow.parser.DateTimeParser().parse('2021-10-12 14:30:00', 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss')\n        datetime.datetime(2021, 10, 12, 14, 30)\n\n\n        \"\"\"\n        if normalize_whitespace:\n            datetime_string = re.sub(r\"\\s+\", \" \", datetime_string)\n\n        if isinstance(fmt, list):\n            return self._parse_multiformat(datetime_string, fmt)\n\n        try:\n            fmt_tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE]\n            fmt_pattern_re: Pattern[str]\n            fmt_tokens, fmt_pattern_re = self._generate_pattern_re(fmt)\n        except re.error as e:\n            raise ParserMatchError(\n                f\"Failed to generate regular expression pattern: {e}.\"\n            )\n\n        match = fmt_pattern_re.search(datetime_string)\n\n        if match is None:\n            raise ParserMatchError(\n                f\"Failed to match {fmt!r} when parsing {datetime_string!r}.\"\n            )\n\n        parts: _Parts = {}\n        for token in fmt_tokens:\n            value: Union[Tuple[str, str, str], str]\n            if token == \"Do\":\n                value = match.group(\"value\")\n            elif token == \"W\":\n                value = (match.group(\"year\"), match.group(\"week\"), match.group(\"day\"))\n            else:\n                value = match.group(token)\n\n            if value is None:\n                raise ParserMatchError(\n                    f\"Unable to find a match group for the specified token {token!r}.\"\n                )\n\n            self._parse_token(token, value, parts)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n\n        return self._build_datetime(parts)\n\n    def _generate_pattern_re(self, fmt: str) -> Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Generates a regular expression pattern from a format string.\n\n        :param fmt: The format string to convert into a regular expression pattern.\n        :type fmt: str\n        :returns: A tuple containing a list of format tokens and the corresponding regular expression pattern.\n        :rtype: Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]\n        :raises ParserError: If an unrecognized token is encountered in the format string.\n        \"\"\"\n        # fmt is a string of tokens like 'YYYY-MM-DD'\n        # we construct a new string by replacing each\n        # token by its pattern:\n        # 'YYYY-MM-DD' -> '(?P<YYYY>\\d{4})-(?P<MM>\\d{2})-(?P<DD>\\d{2})'\n        tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE] = []\n        offset = 0\n\n        # Escape all special RegEx chars\n        escaped_fmt = re.escape(fmt)\n\n        # Extract the bracketed expressions to be reinserted later.\n        escaped_fmt = re.sub(self._ESCAPE_RE, \"#\", escaped_fmt)\n\n        # Any number of S is the same as one.\n        # TODO: allow users to specify the number of digits to parse\n        escaped_fmt = re.sub(r\"S+\", \"S\", escaped_fmt)\n\n        escaped_data = re.findall(self._ESCAPE_RE, fmt)\n\n        fmt_pattern = escaped_fmt\n\n        for m in self._FORMAT_RE.finditer(escaped_fmt):\n            token: _FORMAT_TYPE = cast(_FORMAT_TYPE, m.group(0))\n            try:\n                input_re = self._input_re_map[token]\n            except KeyError:\n                raise ParserError(f\"Unrecognized token {token!r}.\")\n            input_pattern = f\"(?P<{token}>{input_re.pattern})\"\n            tokens.append(token)\n            # a pattern doesn't have the same length as the token\n            # it replaces! We keep the difference in the offset variable.\n            # This works because the string is scanned left-to-right and matches\n            # are returned in the order found by finditer.\n            fmt_pattern = (\n                fmt_pattern[: m.start() + offset]\n                + input_pattern\n                + fmt_pattern[m.end() + offset :]\n            )\n            offset += len(input_pattern) - (m.end() - m.start())\n\n        final_fmt_pattern = \"\"\n        split_fmt = fmt_pattern.split(r\"\\#\")\n\n        # Due to the way Python splits, 'split_fmt' will always be longer\n        for i in range(len(split_fmt)):\n            final_fmt_pattern += split_fmt[i]\n            if i < len(escaped_data):\n                final_fmt_pattern += escaped_data[i][1:-1]\n\n        # Wrap final_fmt_pattern in a custom word boundary to strictly\n        # match the formatting pattern and filter out date and time formats\n        # that include junk such as: blah1998-09-12 blah, blah 1998-09-12blah,\n        # blah1998-09-12blah. The custom word boundary matches every character\n        # that is not a whitespace character to allow for searching for a date\n        # and time string in a natural language sentence. Therefore, searching\n        # for a string of the form YYYY-MM-DD in \"blah 1998-09-12 blah\" will\n        # work properly.\n        # Certain punctuation before or after the target pattern such as\n        # \"1998-09-12,\" is permitted. For the full list of valid punctuation,\n        # see the documentation.\n\n        starting_word_boundary = (\n            r\"(?<!\\S\\S)\"  # Don't have two consecutive non-whitespace characters. This ensures that we allow cases\n            # like .11.25.2019 but not 1.11.25.2019 (for pattern MM.DD.YYYY)\n            r\"(?<![^\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)<>\\s])\"  # This is the list of punctuation that is ok before the\n            # pattern (i.e. \"It can't not be these characters before the pattern\")\n            r\"(\\b|^)\"\n            # The \\b is to block cases like 1201912 but allow 201912 for pattern YYYYMM. The ^ was necessary to allow a\n            # negative number through i.e. before epoch numbers\n        )\n        ending_word_boundary = (\n            r\"(?=[\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)\\<\\>]?\"  # Positive lookahead stating that these punctuation marks\n            # can appear after the pattern at most 1 time\n            r\"(?!\\S))\"  # Don't allow any non-whitespace character after the punctuation\n        )\n        bounded_fmt_pattern = r\"{}{}{}\".format(\n            starting_word_boundary, final_fmt_pattern, ending_word_boundary\n        )\n\n        return tokens, re.compile(bounded_fmt_pattern, flags=re.IGNORECASE)\n\n    @overload\n    def _parse_token(\n        self,\n        token: Literal[\n            \"YYYY\",\n            \"YY\",\n            \"MM\",\n            \"M\",\n            \"DDDD\",\n            \"DDD\",\n            \"DD\",\n            \"D\",\n            \"Do\",\n            \"HH\",\n            \"hh\",\n            \"h\",\n            \"H\",\n            \"mm\",\n            \"m\",\n            \"ss\",\n            \"s\",\n            \"x\",\n        ],\n        value: Union[str, bytes, SupportsInt, bytearray],\n        parts: _Parts,\n    ) -> None: ...  # pragma: no cover\n\n    def _parse_token(\n        self,\n        token: Any,\n        value: Any,\n        parts: _Parts,\n    ) -> None:\n        \"\"\"\n        Parse a token and its value, and update the `_Parts` dictionary with the parsed values.\n\n        The function supports several tokens, including \"YYYY\", \"YY\", \"MMMM\", \"MMM\", \"MM\", \"M\", \"DDDD\", \"DDD\", \"DD\", \"D\", \"Do\", \"dddd\", \"ddd\", \"HH\", \"H\", \"mm\", \"m\", \"ss\", \"s\", \"S\", \"X\", \"x\", \"ZZZ\", \"ZZ\", \"Z\", \"a\", \"A\", and \"W\". Each token is matched and the corresponding value is parsed and added to the `_Parts` dictionary.\n\n        :param token: The token to parse.\n        :type token: Any\n        :param value: The value of the token.\n        :type value: Any\n        :param parts: A dictionary to update with the parsed values.\n        :type parts: _Parts\n        :raises ParserMatchError: If the hour token value is not between 0 and 12 inclusive for tokens \"a\" or \"A\".\n\n        \"\"\"\n        if token == \"YYYY\":\n            parts[\"year\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token == \"YY\":\n            value = int(value)\n            parts[\"year\"] = 1900 + value if value > 68 else 2000 + value\n\n        elif token in [\"MMMM\", \"MMM\"]:\n            # FIXME: month_number() is nullable\n            parts[\"month\"] = self.locale.month_number(value.lower())  # type: ignore[typeddict-item]\n\n        elif token in [\"MM\", \"M\"]:\n            parts[\"month\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"DDDD\", \"DDD\"]:\n            parts[\"day_of_year\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"DD\", \"D\"]:\n            parts[\"day\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token == \"Do\":\n            parts[\"day\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token == \"dddd\":\n            # locale day names are 1-indexed\n            day_of_week = [x.lower() for x in self.locale.day_names].index(\n                value.lower()\n            )\n            parts[\"day_of_week\"] = day_of_week - 1\n\n        elif token == \"ddd\":\n            # locale day abbreviations are 1-indexed\n            day_of_week = [x.lower() for x in self.locale.day_abbreviations].index(\n                value.lower()\n            )\n            parts[\"day_of_week\"] = day_of_week - 1\n\n        elif token.upper() in [\"HH\", \"H\"]:\n            parts[\"hour\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"mm\", \"m\"]:\n            parts[\"minute\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"ss\", \"s\"]:\n            parts[\"second\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token == \"S\":\n            # We have the *most significant* digits of an arbitrary-precision integer.\n            # We want the six most significant digits as an integer, rounded.\n            # IDEA: add nanosecond support somehow? Need datetime support for it first.\n            value = value.ljust(7, \"0\")\n\n            # floating-point (IEEE-754) defaults to half-to-even rounding\n            seventh_digit = int(value[6])\n            if seventh_digit == 5:\n                rounding = int(value[5]) % 2\n            elif seventh_digit > 5:\n                rounding = 1\n            else:\n                rounding = 0\n\n            parts[\"microsecond\"] = int(value[:6]) + rounding\n\n        elif token == \"X\":\n            parts[\"timestamp\"] = float(value)\n\n        elif token == \"x\":\n            parts[\"expanded_timestamp\"] = int(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"ZZZ\", \"ZZ\", \"Z\"]:\n            parts[\"tzinfo\"] = TzinfoParser.parse(value)\n\n        elif token in [\"a\", \"A\"]:\n            if value in (self.locale.meridians[\"am\"], self.locale.meridians[\"AM\"]):\n                parts[\"am_pm\"] = \"am\"\n                if \"hour\" in parts and not 0 <= parts[\"hour\"] <= 12:\n                    raise ParserMatchError(\n                        f\"Hour token value must be between 0 and 12 inclusive for token {token!r}.\"\n                    )\n            elif value in (self.locale.meridians[\"pm\"], self.locale.meridians[\"PM\"]):\n                parts[\"am_pm\"] = \"pm\"\n        elif token == \"W\":\n            parts[\"weekdate\"] = value\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _build_datetime(parts: _Parts) -> datetime:\n        \"\"\"\n        Build a datetime object from a dictionary of date parts.\n\n        :param parts: A dictionary containing the date parts Call with ``end`` alone to\n   437→        return the entire range.  Call with ``limit`` alone to return a maximum # of results from\n   438→        the start.  Call with both to cap a range at a maximum # of results.\n   439→\n   440→        **NOTE**: ``tz`` internally **replaces** the timezones of both ``start`` and ``end`` before\n   441→        iterating.  As such, either call with naive objects and ``tz``, or aware objects from the\n   442→        same timezone and no ``tz``.\n   443→\n   444→        Supported frame values: year, quarter, month, week, day, hour, minute, second, microsecond.\n   445→\n   446→        Recognized datetime expressions:\n   447→\n   448→            - An :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object.\n   449→            - A [2013-05-05T16:30:00+00:00]>\n   463→\n   464→        **NOTE**: Unlike Python's ``range``, ``end`` *may* be included in the returned iterator::\n   465→\n   466→            >>> start = datetime(2013, bounds: _BOUNDS = \"[)\",\n   509→        exact: bool = False,\n   510→        week_start: int = 1,\n   511→    ) -> Tuple[\"Arrow\", \"Arrow\"]:\n   512→        \"\"\"Returns a tuple of two new :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` objects, representing the timespan\n   513→        of the :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object in a given timeframe.\n   514→\n   515→        :param frame: the timeframe.  Can be any ``datetime`` property (day, hour, minute...).\n   516→        :param count: (optional) the number of frames to span.\n   517→        :param bounds: (optional) a ``str`` of either '()', '(]', '[)', or '[]' that specifies\n   518→            whether to include or exclude the start and end values in the span. '(' excludes\n   519→            the start, '[' includes the start, ')' excludes the end, and ']' includes the end.\n   520→            If the bounds are not specified, the default bound '[)' is used.\n   521→        :param exact: (optional) whether to have the start of the timespan begin exactly\n   522→            at the time specified by ``start`` and the end of the timespan truncated\n   523→            so as not to extend beyond ``end``.\n   524→        :param week_start: (optional) only used in combination with the week timeframe. Follows isoweekday() where\n   525→            Monday is 1 and Sunday is 7.\n   526→\n   527→        Supported frame values: year, quarter, month, week, day, hour, minute, second.\n   528→\n   529→        Usage::\n   530→\n   531→            >>> arrow.utcnow()\n   532→            <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:32:36.186203+00:00]>\n   533→\n   534→            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('hour')\n   535→            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T03:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n   536→\n   537→            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('day')\n   538→            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-09T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n   539→\n   540→            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('day', count=2)\n   541→            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T00:00:00+00:00]>, 548→\n   549→            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('week', week_start=6)\n   550→            (<Arrow [2021-02-20T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2021-02-26T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n   551→\n   552→        \"\"\"\n   553→\n   554→        util.validate_bounds(bounds)\n   555→\n   556→        frame_absolute, frame_relative, relative_steps = self._get_frames(frame)\n   557→\n   558→        if \"day\"\n   562→        elif frame_absolute == \"quarter\":\n   563→            attr = \"month\"\n   564→        else:\n   565→            attr = frame_absolute\n   566→\n   567→        floor = self\n   568→        if not exact:\n   569→            index = self._ATTRS.index(attr)\n   570→            frames = self._ATTRS[: index + 1]\n   571→\n   572→            values = [getattr(self, f) for f in frames]\n   573→\n   574→            for _ in range(3 - len(values)):\n   575→                values.append(1)\n   576→\n   577→            floor = self.__class__(*values, tzinfo=self.tzinfo)  # type: ignore[misc]\n   578→\n   579→            if frame_absolute == \"week\":\n   580→                # if week_start is greater than self.isoweekday() go back one week by setting delta = 7\n   581→                delta = 7 if week_start > self.isoweekday() else 0\n   582→                floor = floor.shift(days=-(self.isoweekday() - week_start) - delta)\n   583→            elif frame_absolute == \"quarter\":\n   584→                floor = floor.shift(months=-((self.month - 1) % 3))\n   585→\n   586→        ceil = floor.shift(\n   587→            check_imaginary=True, **{frame_relative: count       (<Arrow [2013-05-05T17:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-05T17:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n   701→\n   702→        \"\"\"\n   703→\n   704→        tzinfo = cls._get_tzinfo(start.tzinfo if tz is None 1208→                    )\n  1209→\n  1210→                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_HOUR * 2:\n  1211→                    return locale.describe(\"hour\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n                else:\n  1286→                    raise ValueError(\n  1287→                        \"Invalid level of granularity. \"\n  1288→                        \"Please select between 'second', 1349→        :param timestring: a ``str`` representing a humanized relative time.\n  1350→        :param locale: (optional) a ``str`` specifying a locale. Can be any ``datetime`` property (day, hour, minute...).\n        :param count: (optional) the number of frames to span.\n        :param bounds: (optional) a ``str`` of either '()', '(]', '[)', or '[]' that specifies\n            whether to include or exclude the start and end values in the span. '(' excludes\n            the start, '[' includes the start, ')' excludes the end, and ']' includes the end.\n            If the bounds are not specified, the default bound '[)' is used.\n        :param exact: (optional) whether to have the start of the timespan begin exactly\n            at the time specified by ``start`` and the end of the timespan truncated\n            so as not to extend beyond ``end``.\n        :param week_start: (optional) only used in combination with the week timeframe. Follows isoweekday() where\n            Monday is 1 and Sunday is 7.\n\n        Supported frame values: year, quarter, month, week, day, hour, minute, second.\n\n        Usage::\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow()\n            <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:32:36.186203+00:00]>\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('hour')\n            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T03:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('day')\n            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-09T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('day', count=2)\n            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-10T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('day', bounds='[]')\n            (<Arrow [2013-05-09T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2013-05-10T00:00:00+00:00]>)\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('week')\n            (<Arrow [2021-02-22T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2021-02-28T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().span('week', week_start=6)\n            (<Arrow [2021-02-20T00:00:00+00:00]>, <Arrow [2021-02-26T23:59:59.999999+00:00]>)\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        util.validate_bounds(bounds)\n\n        frame_absolute, frame_relative, relative_steps = self._get_frames(frame)\n\n        if frame_absolute == \"week\":\n            if not 1 <= week_start <= 7:\n                raise ValueError(\"week_start argument must be between 1 and 7.\")\n            attr = \"day\"\n        elif frame_absolute == \"quarter\":\n            attr = \"month\"\n        else:\n            attr = frame_absolute\n\n        floor = self\n        if not exact:\n            index = self._ATTRS.index(attr)\n            frames = self._ATTRS[: index + 1]\n\n            values = [getattr(self, f) for f in frames]\n\n            for _ in range(3 - <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object with attributes updated\n        according to inputs.\n\n        Parameters:\n        check_imaginary (bool): If True (default), will check for and resolve\n        imaginary times (like during DST transitions). If False, skips this check.\n\n\n        Use pluralized property names to relatively shift their current value:\n\n        >>> import arrow\n        >>> arw = arrow.utcnow()\n        >>> arw\n        <Arrow [2013-05-11T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n        >>> arw.shift(years=1, months=-1)\n        <Arrow [2014-04-11T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n\n        Day-of-the-week relative shifting can use either Python's weekday numbers\n        (Monday = 0, Tuesday = 1 .. Sunday = 6) or using dateutil.relativedelta's\n        day instances (MO, TU .. SU).  When using weekday numbers, the returned\n        date will always be greater than or equal to the starting date.\n\n        Using the above code (which is a Saturday) and asking it to shift to Saturday:\n\n        >>> arw.shift(weekday=5)\n        <Arrow [2013-05-11T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n\n        While asking for a Monday:\n\n        >>> arw.shift(weekday=0)\n        <Arrow [2013-05-13T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        relative_kwargs = {}\n        additional_attrs = [\"weeks\", \"quarters\", \"weekday\"]\n\n        for key, value in kwargs.items():\n            if key in self._ATTRS_PLURAL or key in additional_attrs:\n                relative_kwargs[key] = value\n            else:\n                supported_attr = \", \".join(self._ATTRS_PLURAL + additional_attrs)\n                raise ValueError(\n                    f\"Invalid shift time frame. Please select one of the following: {supported_attr}.\"\n                )\n\n        # core datetime does not support quarters, translate to months.\n        relative_kwargs.setdefault(\"months\", 0)\n        relative_kwargs[\"months\"] += (\n            relative_kwargs.pop(\"quarters\", 0) * self._MONTHS_PER_QUARTER\n        )\n\n        current = self._datetime + relativedelta(**relative_kwargs)\n\n        # If check_imaginary is True, perform the check for imaginary times (DST transitions)\n        if check_imaginary and not dateutil_tz.datetime_exists(current):\n            current = dateutil_tz.resolve_imaginary(current)\n\n        return self.fromdatetime(current)\n\n    def to(self, tz: TZ_EXPR) -> \"Arrow\":\n        \"\"\"Returns a new :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object, converted\n        to the target timezone.\n\n        :param tz: A :ref:`timezone expression <tz-expr>`.\n\n        Usage::\n\n            >>> utc = arrow.utcnow()\n            >>> utc\n            <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:49:12.311072+00:00]>\n\n            >>> utc.to('US/Pacific')\n            <Arrow [2013-05-08T20:49:12.311072-07:00]>\n\n            >>> utc.to(tz.tzlocal())\n            <Arrow [2013-05-08T20:49:12.311072-07:00]>\n\n            >>> utc.to('-07:00')\n            <Arrow [2013-05-08T20:49:12.311072-07:00]>\n\n            >>> utc.to('local')\n            <Arrow [2013-05-08T20:49:12.311072-07:00]>\n\n            >>> utc.to('local').to('utc')\n            <Arrow [2013-05-09T03:49:12.311072+00:00]>\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if not isinstance(tz, dt_tzinfo):\n            tz = parser.TzinfoParser.parse(tz)\n\n        dt = self._datetime.astimezone(tz)\n\n        return self.__class__(\n            dt.year,\n            dt.month,\n            dt.day,\n            dt.hour,\n            dt.minute,\n            dt.second,\n            dt.microsecond,\n            dt.tzinfo,\n            fold=getattr(dt, \"fold\", 0),\n        )\n\n    # string output and formatting\n\n    def format(\n        self, fmt: str = \"YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ssZZ\", locale: str = DEFAULT_LOCALE\n    ) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Returns a string representation of the :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object,\n        formatted according to the provided format string. For a list of formatting values,\n        see :ref:`supported-tokens`\n\n        :param fmt: the format string.\n        :param locale: the locale to format.\n\n        Usage::\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().format('YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss ZZ')\n            '2013-05-09 03:56:47 -00:00'\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().format('X')\n            '1368071882'\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().format('MMMM DD, YYYY')\n            'May 09, 2013'\n\n            >>> arrow.utcnow().format()\n            '2013-05-09 03:56:47 -00:00'\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return formatter.DateTimeFormatter(locale).format(self._datetime, fmt)\n\n    def humanize(\n        self,\n        other: Union[\"Arrow\", dt_datetime, None] = None,\n        locale: str = DEFAULT_LOCALE,\n        only_distance: bool = False,\n        granularity: Union[_GRANULARITY, List[_GRANULARITY]] = \"auto\",\n    ) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Returns a localized, humanized representation of a relative difference in time.\n\n        :param other: (optional) an :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` or ``datetime`` object.\n            Defaults to now in the current :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object's timezone.\n        :param locale: (optional) a ``str`` specifying a locale.  Defaults to 'en-us'.\n        :param only_distance: (optional) returns only time difference eg: \"11 seconds\" without \"in\" or \"ago\" part.\n        :param granularity: (optional) defines the precision of the output. Set it to strings 'second', 'minute',\n                           'hour', 'day', 'week', 'month' or 'year' or a list of any combination of these strings\n\n        Usage::\n\n            >>> earlier = arrow.utcnow().shift(hours=-2)\n            >>> earlier.humanize()\n            '2 hours ago'\n\n            >>> later = earlier.shift(hours=4)\n            >>> later.humanize(earlier)\n            'in 4 hours'\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        locale_name = locale\n        locale = locales.get_locale(locale)\n\n        if other is None:\n            utc = dt_datetime.now(timezone.utc).replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n            dt = utc.astimezone(self._datetime.tzinfo)\n\n        elif isinstance(other, Arrow):\n            dt = other._datetime\n\n        elif isinstance(other, dt_datetime):\n            if other.tzinfo is None:\n                dt = other.replace(tzinfo=self._datetime.tzinfo)\n            else:\n                dt = other.astimezone(self._datetime.tzinfo)\n\n        else:\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"Invalid 'other' argument of type {type(other).__name__!r}. \"\n                \"Argument must be of type None, Arrow, or datetime.\"\n            )\n\n        if isinstance(granularity, list) and len(granularity) == 1:\n            granularity = granularity[0]\n\n        _delta = int(round((self._datetime - dt).total_seconds()))\n        sign = -1 if _delta < 0 else 1\n        delta_second = diff = abs(_delta)\n\n        try:\n            if granularity == \"auto\":\n                if diff < 10:\n                    return locale.describe(\"now\", only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                if diff < self._SECS_PER_MINUTE:\n                    seconds = sign * delta_second\n                    return locale.describe(\n                        \"seconds\", seconds, only_distance=only_distance\n                    )\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_MINUTE * 2:\n                    return locale.describe(\"minute\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_HOUR:\n                    minutes = sign * max(delta_second // self._SECS_PER_MINUTE, 2)\n                    return locale.describe(\n                        \"minutes\", minutes, only_distance=only_distance\n                    )\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_HOUR * 2:\n                    return locale.describe(\"hour\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_DAY:\n                    hours = sign * max(delta_second // self._SECS_PER_HOUR, 2)\n                    return locale.describe(\"hours\", hours, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                calendar_diff = (\n                    relativedelta(dt, self._datetime)\n                    if self._datetime < dt\n                    else relativedelta(self._datetime, dt)\n                )\n                calendar_months = (\n                    calendar_diff.years * self._MONTHS_PER_YEAR + calendar_diff.months\n                )\n\n                # For months, if more than 2 weeks, count as a full month\n                if calendar_diff.days > 14:\n                    calendar_months += 1\n\n                calendar_months = min(calendar_months, self._MONTHS_PER_YEAR)\n\n                if diff < self._SECS_PER_DAY * 2:\n                    return locale.describe(\"day\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_WEEK:\n                    days = sign * max(delta_second // self._SECS_PER_DAY, 2)\n                    return locale.describe(\"days\", days, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif calendar_months >= 1 and diff < self._SECS_PER_YEAR:\n                    if calendar_months == 1:\n                        return locale.describe(\n                            \"month\", sign, only_distance=only_distance\n                        )\n                    else:\n                        months = sign * calendar_months\n                        return locale.describe(\n                            \"months\", months, only_distance=only_distance\n                        )\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_WEEK * 2:\n                    return locale.describe(\"week\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_MONTH:\n                    weeks = sign * max(delta_second // self._SECS_PER_WEEK, 2)\n                    return locale.describe(\"weeks\", weeks, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                elif diff < self._SECS_PER_YEAR * 2:\n                    return locale.describe(\"year\", sign, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n                else:\n                    years = sign * max(delta_second // self._SECS_PER_YEAR, 2)\n                    return locale.describe(\"years\", years, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n            elif isinstance(granularity, str):\n                granularity = cast(TimeFrameLiteral, granularity)  # type: ignore[assignment]\n\n                if granularity == \"second\":\n                    delta = sign * float(delta_second)\n                    if abs(delta) < 2:\n                        return locale.describe(\"now\", only_distance=only_distance)\n                elif granularity == \"minute\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_MINUTE\n                elif granularity == \"hour\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_HOUR\n                elif granularity == \"day\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_DAY\n                elif granularity == \"week\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_WEEK\n                elif granularity == \"month\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_MONTH\n                elif granularity == \"quarter\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_QUARTER\n                elif granularity == \"year\":\n                    delta = sign * delta_second / self._SECS_PER_YEAR\n                else:\n                    raise ValueError(\n                        \"Invalid level of granularity. \"\n                        \"Please select between 'second', 'minute', 'hour', 'day', 'week', 'month', 'quarter' or 'year'.\"\n                    )\n\n                if trunc(abs(delta)) != 1:\n                    granularity += \"s\"  # type: ignore[assignment]\n                return locale.describe(granularity, delta, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n            else:\n                if not granularity:\n                    raise ValueError(\n                        \"Empty granularity list provided. \"\n                        \"Please select one or more from 'second', 'minute', 'hour', 'day', 'week', 'month', 'quarter', 'year'.\"\n                    )\n\n                timeframes: List[Tuple[TimeFrameLiteral, float]] = []\n\n                def gather_timeframes(_delta: float, _frame: TimeFrameLiteral) -> float:\n                    if _frame in granularity:\n                        value = sign * _delta / self._SECS_MAP[_frame]\n                        _delta %= self._SECS_MAP[_frame]\n                        if trunc(abs(value)) != 1:\n                            timeframes.append(\n                                (cast(TimeFrameLiteral, _frame + \"s\"), value)\n                            )\n                        else:\n                            timeframes.append((_frame, value))\n                    return _delta\n\n                delta = float(delta_second)\n                frames: Tuple[TimeFrameLiteral, ...] = (\n                    \"year\",\n                    \"quarter\",\n                    \"month\",\n                    \"week\",\n                    \"day\",\n                    \"hour\",\n                    \"minute\",\n                    \"second\",\n                )\n                for frame in frames:\n                    delta = gather_timeframes(delta, frame)\n\n                if len(timeframes) < len(granularity):\n                    raise ValueError(\n                        \"Invalid level of granularity. \"\n                        \"Please select between 'second', 'minute', 'hour', 'day', 'week', 'month', 'quarter' or 'year'.\"\n                    )\n\n                return locale.describe_multi(timeframes, only_distance=only_distance)\n\n        except KeyError as e:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"Humanization of the {e} granularity is not currently translated in the {locale_name!r} locale. \"\n                \"Please consider making a contribution to this locale.\"\n            )\n\n    def dehumanize(self, input_string: str, locale: str = \"en_us\") -> \"Arrow\":\n        \"\"\"Returns a new :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object, that represents\n        the time difference relative to the attributes of the\n        :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object.\n\n        :param timestring: a ``str`` representing a humanized relative time.\n        :param locale: (optional) a ``str`` specifying a locale.  Defaults to 'en-us'.\n\n        Usage::\n\n                >>> arw = arrow.utcnow()\n                >>> arw\n                <Arrow [2021-04-20T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n                >>> earlier = arw.dehumanize(\"2 days ago\")\n                >>> earlier\n                <Arrow [2021-04-18T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n\n                >>> arw = arrow.utcnow()\n                >>> arw\n                <Arrow [2021-04-20T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n                >>> later = arw.dehumanize(\"in a month\")\n                >>> later\n                <Arrow [2021-05-18T22:27:34.787885+00:00]>\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Create a locale object based off given local\n        locale_obj = locales.get_locale(locale)\n\n        # Check to see if locale is supported\n        normalized_locale_name = locale.lower().replace(\"_\", \"-\")\n\n        if normalized_locale_name not in DEHUMANIZE_LOCALES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"Dehumanize does not currently support the {locale} locale, please consider making a contribution to add support for this locale.\"\n            )\n\n        current_time = self.fromdatetime(self._datetime)\n\n        # Create an object containing the relative time info\n        time_object_info = dict.fromkeys(\n            [\"seconds\", \"minutes\", \"hours\", \"days\", \"weeks\", \"months\", \"years\"], 0\n        )\n\n        # Create an object representing if unit has been seen\n        unit_visited = dict.fromkeys(\n            [\"now\", \"seconds\", \"minutes\", \"hours\", \"days\", \"weeks\", \"months\", \"years\"],\n            False,\n        )\n\n        # Create a regex pattern object for numbers\n        num_pattern = re.compile(r\"\\d+\")\n\n        # Search input string for each time unit within locale\n        for unit, unit_object in locale_obj.timeframes.items():\n            # Need to check the type of unit_object to create the correct dictionary\n            if isinstance(unit_object, Mapping):\n                strings_to_search = unit_object\n            else:\n                strings_to_search = {unit: str(unit_object)}\n\n            # Search for any matches that exist for that locale's unit.\n            # Needs to cycle all through strings as some locales have strings that\n            # could overlap in a regex match, since input validation isn't being performed.\n            for time_delta, time_string in strings_to_search.items():\n                # Replace {0} with regex \\d representing digits\n                search_string = str(time_string)\n                search_string = search_string.format(r\"\\d+\")\n\n                # Create search pattern and find within string\n                pattern = re.compile(rf\"(^|\\b|\\d){search_string}\")\n                match = pattern.search(input_string)\n\n                # If there is no match continue to next iteration\n                if not match:\n                    continue\n\n                match_string = match.group()\n                num_match = num_pattern.search(match_string)\n\n                # If no number matches\n                # Need for absolute value as some locales have signs included in their objects\n                if not num_match:\n                    change_value = (\n                        1 if not time_delta.isnumeric() else abs(int(time_delta))\n                    )\n                else:\n                    change_value = int(num_match.group())\n\n                # No time to update if now is the unit\n                if unit == \"now\":\n                    unit_visited[unit] = True\n                    continue\n\n                # Add change value to the correct unit (incorporates the plurality that exists within timeframe i.e second v.s seconds)\n                time_unit_to_change = str(unit)\n                time_unit_to_change += (\n                    \"s\" if (str(time_unit_to_change)[-1] != \"s\") else \"\"\n                )\n                time_object_info[time_unit_to_change] = change_value\n                unit_visited[time_unit_to_change] = True\n\n        # Assert error if string does not modify any units\n        if not any([True for k, v in unit_visited.items() if v]):\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Input string not valid. Note: Some locales do not support the week granularity in Arrow. \"\n                \"If you are attempting to use the week granularity on an unsupported locale, this could be the cause of this error.\"\n            )\n\n        # Sign logic\n        future_string = locale_obj.future\n        future_string = future_string.format(\".*\")\n        future_pattern = re.compile(rf\"^{future_string}$\")\n        future_pattern_match = future_pattern.findall(input_string)\n\n        past_string = locale_obj.past\n        past_string = past_string.format(\".*\")\n        past_pattern = re.compile(rf\"^{past_string}$\")\n        past_pattern_match = past_pattern.findall(input_string)\n\n        # If a string contains the now unit, there will be no relative units, hence the need to check if the now unit\n        # was visited before raising a ValueError\n        if past_pattern_match:\n            sign_val = -1\n        elif future_pattern_match:\n            sign_val = 1\n        elif unit_visited[\"now\"]:\n            sign_val = 0\n        else:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Invalid input String. String does not contain any relative time information. \"\n                \"String should either represent a time in the future or a time in the past. \"\n                \"Ex: 'in 5 seconds' or '5 seconds ago'.\"\n            )\n\n        time_changes = {k: sign_val * v for k, v in time_object_info.items()}\n\n        return current_time.shift(check_imaginary=True, **time_changes)\n\n    # query functions\n\n    def is_between(\n        self,\n        start: \"Arrow\",\n        end: \"Arrow\",\n        bounds: _BOUNDS = \"()\",\n    ) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Returns a boolean denoting whether the :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object is between\n        the start and end limits.\n\n        :param start: an :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object.\n        :param end: an :class:`Arrow <arrow.arrow.Arrow>` object.\n        :param bounds: (optional) a ``str`` of either '()', '(]', '[)', or '[]' that specifies\n            whether to include or exclude the start and end values in the range. '(' excludes\n            the start, '[' includes the start, ')' excludes the end, and ']' includes the end.\n            If the bounds are not specified, the default bound '()' is used.\n\n        Usage::\n\n            >>> start = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 5, 12, 30, 10))\n            >>> end = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 5, 12, 30, 36))\n            >>> arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 5, 12, 30, 27)).is_between(start, end)\n            True\n\n            >>> start = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 5))\n            >>> end = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 8))\n            >>> arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 8)).is_between(start, end, '[]')\n            True\n\n            >>> start = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 5))\n            >>> end = arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 8))\n            >>> arrow.get(datetime(2013, 5, 8)).is_between(start, end, '[)')\n            False\n\n        \"\"\"\n\n        util.validate_bounds(bounds)\n\n        if not isinstance(start, Arrow):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"Cannot parse start date argument type of {type(start)!r}.\"\n            )\n\n        if not isinstance(end, Arrow):\n            raise TypeError(f\"Cannot parse end date argument type of {type(start)!r}.\")\n\n        include_start = bounds[0] == \"[\"\n        include_end = bounds[1] == \"]\"\n\n        target_ts = self.float_timestamp\n        start_ts = start.float_timestamp\n        end_ts = end.float_timestamp\n\n        return (\n            (start_ts <= target_ts <= end_ts)\n            and (include_start or start_ts < target_ts)\n            and (include_end or target_ts < end_ts)\n        )\n\n    # datetime methods\n\n    def date(self) -> date:\n        \"\"\"Returns a ``date`` object with the same year, month and day.\n\n
